=== PROMPT VARIANT ===
r1

=== PROMPT ===
You will be given a competitive programming problem.
You are given an array of integers nums of size n and a positive integer threshold.
There is a graph consisting of n nodes with the i^th node having a value of nums[i]. Two nodes i and j in the graph are connected via an undirected edge if lcm(nums[i], nums[j]) <= threshold.
Return the number of connected components in this graph.
A connected component is a subgraph of a graph in which there exists a path between any two vertices, and no vertex of the subgraph shares an edge with a vertex outside of the subgraph.
The term lcm(a, b) denotes the least common multiple of a and b.
 
Example 1:

Input: nums = [2,4,8,3,9], threshold = 5
Output: 4
Explanation: 

 
The four connected components are (2, 4), (3), (8), (9).

Example 2:

Input: nums = [2,4,8,3,9,12], threshold = 10
Output: 2
Explanation: 

The two connected components are (2, 3, 4, 8, 9), and (12).

 
Constraints:

1 <= nums.length <= 10^5
1 <= nums[i] <= 10^9
All elements of nums are unique.
1 <= threshold <= 2 * 10^5

Use the following starter code to write the final solution.
```python
class Solution:
    def countComponents(self, nums: List[int], threshold: int) -> int:
        
```

Reason in a commit-implement-recheck way.

A productive rhythm for this task usually looks like this:
- Isolate the decisive constraints, the target complexity, and at most one or two approaches that are still truly plausible.
- If two candidates remain, compare them only on the decisive constraint and commit quickly to the stronger one instead of keeping a wide search open.
- Before coding, keep only a tiny implementation ledger for the chosen plan: main invariant, key state or transition, and the one or two edge cases most likely to fail.
- Move into code as soon as that ledger is clear. After coding starts, keep reasoning attached to the current draft rather than reopening the high-level search.
- After the first complete draft, do one short implementation-check-adjust-check loop on the same draft: test the key edge case, patch locally if needed, and recheck.
- Reopen the main design only if a concrete counterexample breaks the core invariant. Otherwise prefer the local patch and keep closing.
- Once the current draft, invariant, and short recheck still agree, finalize the solution.

Output only the final Python code enclosed in a code block.

Option:
parent = list(range(T+1))
rank = [0]*(T+1)
def find(x):
    while parent[x] != x:
        parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
        x = parent[x]
    return x
def union(a,b):
    ra = find(a); rb = find(b)
    if ra == rb: return
    if rank[ra] < rank[rb]:
        ra, rb = rb, ra
    parent[rb] = ra
    if rank[ra] == rank[rb]:
        rank[ra] += 1

Rank array memory 1.6MB refs to ints? [0]*(T+1) uses same int 0 refs, but when updated creates ints. Fine. Could use size instead. Rank okay.

Let's write code:

from typing import List

class Solution:
    def countComponents(self, nums: List[int], threshold: int) -> int:
        T = threshold
        present = [False] * (T + 1)
        for x in nums:
            if x <= T:
                present[x] = True

        parent = list(range(T + 1))
        rank = [0] * (T + 1)

        def find(x: int) -> int:
            while parent[x] != x:
                parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
                x = parent[x]
            return x

        def union(a: int, b: int) -> bool:
            ra = find(a)
            rb = find(b)
            if ra == rb:
                return False
            if rank[ra] < rank[rb]:
                ra, rb = rb, ra
            parent[rb] = ra
            if rank[ra] == rank[rb]:
                rank[ra] += 1
            return True

        components = len(nums)
        first = [0] * (T + 1)

        for d in range(1, T + 1):
            if not present[d]:
                continue
            for m in range(d, T + 1, d):
                a = first[m]
                if a == 0:
                    first[m] = d
                else:
                    if union(a, d):
                        components -= 1

        return components

Wait, is `components = len(nums)` correct if nums contains values >T and values <=T? Yes. But if a value <=T is not present? no. We only decrement on union between present values. Initial components = number of nodes = len(nums). Each successful union connects two previously disconnected components, reducing count by 1. Since graph has n nodes. Good.

But let's double-check: Suppose union(a,d) where a and d are already connected indirectly, returns False, no decrement. Good.

Now, is there any chance `union(a,d)` called with `a` not a node? `first[m]` is set to d only if present[d]. So a present. Good.

We can implement DSU with parent as list and use iterative find:
def find(x):
    while parent[x] != x:
        parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
        x = parent[x]
    return x

This is standard. 2.2M unions -> 4.4M find calls. Each find maybe while loop. Fine.

The least common multiple lcm(a,d) is the smallest positive common multiple, so lcm(a,d) <= m. Since m <= T, lcm <= T. Thus edge exists. Good. So no false.

So first[m] cannot be d before. During processing d, for each m, if first[m]==0 set to d. If first[m]!=0, it's a smaller divisor. So a != d. Good.

```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def countComponents(self, nums: List[int], threshold: int) -> int:
        T = threshold
        present = [False] * (T + 1)
        for x in nums:
            if x <= T:
                present[x] = True

        parent = list(range(T + 1))
        rank = [0] * (T + 1)

        def find(x: int) -> int:
            while parent[x] != x:
                parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
                x = parent[x]
            return x

        def union(a: int, b: int) -> bool:
            ra = find(a)
            rb = find(b)
            if ra == rb:
                return False
            if rank[ra] < rank[rb]:
                ra, rb = rb, ra
            parent[rb] = ra
            if rank[ra] == rank[rb]:
                rank[ra] += 1
            return True

        components = len(nums)
        first = [0] * (T + 1)

        for d in range(1, T + 1):
            if not present[d]:
                continue
            for m in range(d, T + 1, d):
                a = first[m]
                if a == 0:
                    first[m] = d
                else:
                    if union(a, d):
                        components -= 1

        return components
```

</think>

=== ANSWER ===
```python
from typing import List

class Solution:
    def countComponents(self, nums: List[int], threshold: int) -> int:
        T = threshold

        present = [False] * (T + 1)
        for x in nums:
            if x <= T:
                present[x] = True

        parent = list(range(T + 1))
        rank = [0] * (T + 1)

        def find(x: int) -> int:
            while parent[x] != x:
                parent[x] = parent[parent[x]]
                x = parent[x]
            return x

        def union(a: int, b: int) -> bool:
            ra = find(a)
            rb = find(b)
            if ra == rb:
                return False
            if rank[ra] < rank[rb]:
                ra, rb = rb, ra
            parent[rb] = ra
            if rank[ra] == rank[rb]:
                rank[ra] += 1
            return True

        components = len(nums)

        # first[m] = first present divisor of m seen so far.
        # For every m <= T, all present divisors of m are connected,
        # because any two divisors of m have lcm <= m <= T.
        first = [0] * (T + 1)

        for d in range(1, T + 1):
            if not present[d]:
                continue
            for m in range(d, T + 1, d):
                a = first[m]
                if a == 0:
                    first[m] = d
                else:
                    if union(a, d):
                        components -= 1

        return components
```
